The teaser was therefore **copied** (not moved, so the cards keep theirs) into `field_public_description`, which was put on those displays in its place; `wind_direction` pages still render 156 and 142 chars of body. **Verified 5/5 on live:** 0 view displays show it, 32 forms keep it, 14 view displays still consume it for cards, the re-add attempt is refused, and **no term anywhere is left without a body**. Cards and lists unchanged — `/material`'s Irrigation teaser and the Alkaline-Tolerant list text both still render, while the Alkaline-Tolerant *page* now shows its body and **not** its teaser. **The rule is written into `Governance/ui_patterns.md` and summarised in CLAUDE.md's UI Patterns**, with the corollaries that have each been got wrong once: a card never shows a truncated body; an empty teaser renders nothing; never promote a teaser to cover a missing body; core `description` is unused on public vocabularies; a vocabulary may hold a role under another field name (`services`). **And the conflict rule that caused this:** when a marketing brief and the content model disagree, **the model wins — raise it, do not implement it.**

- **2026-10-03** — **Every public vocabulary now has the same three content fields, all Full HTML (LIVE).** Todd's instruction, to stop marketing and Code having to ask which fields a vocabulary has before writing or pasting. **All 31 public-facing vocabularies** (the `bos_breadcrumbs` allowlist — operational vocabularies deliberately untouched, since ~1,300 operational term pages exist and three copy fields on each would bloat every form for no reader) now carry **`field_short_description` + `field_public_description` + `field_teammate_description`**, configured identically: `text_long`, cardinality 1, **optional**, **`allowed_formats: ['full_html']`**, `text_textarea` widget at 5 rows, and consistent labels (Short Description / Public Description / **Teammate Instructions**). **57 instances created, 8 relabelled, 12 format settings pinned, 2 made optional, 10 form displays created, 57 widgets added, and 105 stored values normalised to Full HTML.** **Four things worth recording.** (1) **`allowed_formats` was the setting marketing flagged as the one that bites** — a field left open to a more restrictive format renders pasted HTML as escaped text and looks like a copy problem rather than a field problem. Pinned to `full_html` everywhere, and **every stored value converted too**, which only ever loosens filtering so no markup is lost. (2) **`required` was normalised DOWN, not up:** `material_types` was the only vocabulary with its public description required, and required would block the office saving a new term before its copy exists — new terms routinely arrive first. All 38 already carry one, so nothing was lost. (3) **Ten vocabularies had no form-display config at all**, falling back to Drupal's implicit default — editable, but not explicit and not uniform, so the display was created for each. (4) **`services` is the one principled exception:** its public and crew copy live in purpose-built `field_service_public_desc` (which has a summary) and `field_service_crew_desc`, so it received only the teaser it genuinely lacked — adding the generic pair alongside would recreate the competing-body-fields problem just cleaned up. **Two items from marketing's resent brief that the earlier pass had not covered, now done:** `field_short_description` was **hidden on all five `plant_characteristics` view modes** and is now visible above the body (that one I had missed — the lists were wired to it while the term pages still could not show it), and the **field configuration is now verified against `material_types`** rather than assumed. `field_call_to_action` was **not** added to `plant_characteristics`, per the deliberate decision that 41 leaf pages each closing with an ask is the thing being avoided. **Verified 10/10 on dev and again on live, including the test marketing asked for before trusting it:** a paragraph of HTML pasted into a real term renders as **real paragraphs with the bold and the link intact and nothing escaped**, and the term was then restored. Six public pages 200 with **0** escaped tags after a 105-value format change. Scripts: `standardize_vocab_content_fields.php`, `verify_vocab_field_uniformity.php` (both idempotent, dry-run default).

- **2026-10-03** — **Core `description` retired on `plant_characteristics` + `growth_zone` — 67 terms migrated, then the nine list views wired to the teaser (LIVE).** Both of marketing's decisions executed in one pass. **Why it was a prerequisite, not tidiness:** those two vocabularies never got the `field_public_description` instance, so their public copy sat in core `description` — one plain field, which cannot hold the public/teammate/short model, and which left the written characteristic bodies with nowhere to go. **Order held exactly, because of the known trap** (core `description` is the source of the `[term:description]` taxonomy metatag default, so clearing it first strips every meta description): **copy → verify identical → repoint displays/views + seed metatags → verify 67 of 67 meta descriptions render → only then clear.** Live DB backed up first (`~/pre-core-desc-migration-20261003.sql.gz.gz`, 147M, gzip-verified). **Result: 67 of 67 byte-identical with intact markup, 0 truncated, 0 damaged**, verified against the source values the migration recorded. Core `description` now empty on all 67 and removed from every view display and the term form — **note it is a BASE field, so it can be emptied and hidden but never deleted per vocabulary**, which is what "retired" means here. `field_meta_tags` added to `growth_zone` (it had none). **The meta seeder gained a body fallback**, because seeding from the teaser alone would have left **40 of 41** characteristic pages with no meta description — and the fallback takes whole opening **sentences** up to ~160 chars rather than a verbatim body, which would have stored 970 characters for Vining. **Decision 2:** the nine list views (8 × `char_cat_*` + `plant_characteristic_children`) now source `field_short_description` with **no trim, no summary and no body fallback** — an empty entry renders nothing, because a silent fallback is what hid this for weeks. Alkaline-Tolerant now reads *"Handles soil above pH 7.5 without going chlorotic…"* and **no longer says "shrubs" on a plant characteristic**; the admin and landing views (`admin_material_plant_characteristics`, `admin_hardiness_zones`, `land_growth_zone`) were repointed at the migrated field instead, so those pages look unchanged. Also cleared the `material_types` stray (Blocks and Pavers). **20/20 verification on dev and again on live**, rendering pages anonymously; all 8 category pages 200 with their terms listed and **0 stale text**; `material_types` (38) and `plant_character_categories` (8) untouched. **⚠ Two things to report honestly.** (1) **The boilerplate count did NOT drop by nine — it is still 15, and it was never going to.** Those 41 characteristic bodies never matched any of the five `BOILERPLATE_PHRASES`: *"Alkaline-tolerant plants and shrubs thrive in soils…"* contains none of them. The 15 are `material_types` 12 + `services` 2 + `deer_protection` 1. The copy was generic and wrong without being *that* flavour of generic, which is a real gap in the phrase list rather than in the migration. (2) **Only 1 of the 9 Environmental Tolerance terms could be pasted** — `Plant Characteristics - Environmental Tolerance 9 Terms.md` was not supplied, and the only text available was the Alkaline-Tolerant teaser quoted in the brief. The other eight are listed in `seed_environmental_tolerance_copy.php` as awaiting copy rather than invented; the real nine were confirmed from the category itself (Acid-Loving, Alkaline-Tolerant, Cold-Hardy, Drought-Tolerant, Heat-Tolerant, Salt-Tolerant, Shade-Tolerant, Sun-Loving, Well-Drained Soil — my first guess had included a non-existent "Wind-Tolerant"). Scripts: `migrate_vocab_core_description_to_public.php`, `repoint_characteristic_description_sources.php`, `clear_material_types_stray_description.php`, `seed_environmental_tolerance_copy.php`, `verify_core_description_retirement.php`, plus the extended `seed_vocab_meta_descriptions_from_short.php` and the existing `retire_vocab_core_description.php` (0 refused on all 67).

- **2026-10-03** — **`bos_content_coverage` — a report that answers "what copy is actually live?" (LIVE).** Built because marketing and Code each asserted the wrong thing about live content within two days, in opposite directions: marketing's copy files carried cross-links written against the designed structure (`/deciduous/fruit-trees`) rather than the live alias (`/deciduous/fruit`), and Code, working from an extract, concluded the `/material` view header was not live when it was and nearly re-pasted it. Neither a copy file nor a build log is the database. **`/admin/office/content-coverage`** (Office → Content coverage; perm `view content coverage report`, office roles on install, **anon 403 verified over real HTTP**) lists all **355 terms across 31 vocabularies** with: Term · Vocabulary · Parent · **Live URL alias** · Teaser · Public description · CTA · Crew description · **Boilerplate flag** · Changed. Copy columns read `n/a` / `EMPTY` / a **plain-text** char count (so the number is words, not markup), with `⚠` on a flagged field — and **`n/a` is deliberately not a gap**, because a vocabulary that never had a teaser slot is not a page missing one. Exposed filters: vocabulary, carries boilerplate, missing public description, missing teaser, changed-before. **Coverage reuses `bos_breadcrumbs::ALLOWED_VIDS`** rather than defining a second list — the repo already had one considered answer to "renders a public page", and it is an allowlist because ~1,300 operational term pages are publicly indexed. **`FIELD_OVERRIDES` is what makes it trustworthy:** `services` keeps its copy in `field_service_public_desc`/`field_service_crew_desc`, so a report knowing only the four default field names would have shown **all 60 service pages as having no copy** — and the override caught **2 flagged service terms my own hand count had missed**. **Boilerplate-flagged at build time: 15 terms** — material_types 12 (the plant-category bodies and crew copy: Plants, Shrubs, Trees, Evergreens, Annuals, Perennials, Groundcovers, Ferns, Grasses, Vines, Roses, plus PVC's crew), services 2 (Patios, Lighting), deer_protection 1. Also 1 missing public description (In House Task) and 53 missing teasers (plant_characteristics 40, growth_zone 13). **Link check shipped too** (`drush bos:content:linkcheck`): **152 internal links across the 355 terms — 147 resolve, 2 via a 301, 3 do not** (a dead property-photo URL on `/services/sprinkler-system/design` in both its public and crew copy, and `/contact-us` which should be `/contact`). **Two gotchas cost two rebuilds and are written up:** the vocabulary scope filter silently returned **995 rows instead of 355 with NO WHERE clause**, because (1) **an exposed filter with no input does not filter**, so a hard scope cannot live on one — the view now has a non-exposed `vid` floor plus a separate exposed `vid`, the billing-views pattern; and (2) **`views`' `Bundle` filter extends `InOperator`, so its operator is `in`** — the rule I recorded on 2026-09-27 for `list_string` filters (only `or`/`and`/`not` emit SQL, because those extend `ManyToOne`) does **not** transfer, and applying it from memory was the wrong move twice. **A third self-inflicted one:** the link checker's first pattern used a `#` delimiter with `#` inside its character class, so it never compiled — the identical bug that once made an audit report "every link resolves" having checked zero. It now throws on a compile failure and fails loudly on zero links, and it separates **redirects from breakage** (`/lighting` answers 301 and works; calling it broken would have sent marketing after two links that are fine). Docs: `Modules/bos_content_coverage.md`.

- **2026-10-03** — **The sixteen root material category descriptions pasted — written 26 September, never applied (LIVE).** `/material`'s sixteen root category pages had been serving generic copy (*"At Brookstone Outdoors, our … category"*, *"carries a complete line of…"*) since the catalogue was built, while finished two-paragraph replacements for every one sat in `Materials Landing Page Copy.md` for a week. All **16 `field_public_description` values** replaced. **The no-collateral-damage claim is proven, not asserted:** a before/after snapshot of **all 38 `material_types` terms** across four copy fields shows **16 bodies changed, 0 teasers, 0 CTAs, 0 crew descriptions** — so the card teasers pasted earlier the same day are byte-for-byte intact — and **Trees / Shrubs / Plants / Annuals untouched**, excluded because the source predates the Plants reparent and still describes them as root categories. **Pre-flight settled the two name questions the brief raised:** the live term is **"Blocks and Pavers"** (plural) where the document said *Block*, so the loader's expected names are the LIVE ones and a mismatch is skipped rather than guessed; and all 16 aliases resolve to exactly one `material_types` term each. **All 16 fields were already `full_html` with no HTML restriction**, and the loader preserves the stored format, so the markup renders as paragraphs — verified positively by finding six distinctive new sentences each **inside a `<p>` element**, not merely by the absence of `&lt;p&gt;`. Verified anonymously: **16/16 pages 200, 0 boilerplate, 0 escaped markup**; both embedded links (`/services/backflow-prevention`, `/services/christmas-decorations`) resolve **200** and are present in their bodies; and all **17 cards on `/material` still show their teaser rather than the new long body** — which is the point of the 10-03 card-hook change. **⚠ Reported, deliberately not built:** `bulk_material` and `supplies` have **no `material_types` term at all** — not unpublished, never created — so those two product lines have no public page. There are **22 material bundles, 20 claimed by a term, 2 unclaimed** (the brief said eighteen bundles; the live count is 22). Terms not created pending Todd's decision; marketing's copy for both is held. **Also still owed from that source file:** the improved `/material` view header and footer, which were not in the supplied extract. Scripts: `seed_root_category_descriptions.php` (dry-run default, `BOS_DESC_APPLY=1`, alias-resolved + name-verified, format-preserving, backs up each previous body plus a teaser snapshot) and `snapshot_material_type_copy.php` (the before/after prover).

- **2026-10-03** — **The last 23 category teasers — every card in the materials catalogue now carries written copy (LIVE).** 7 plant categories (Perennials, Annuals, Grasses, Groundcovers, Ferns, Vines, and Roses on the Shrubs page) + all **16 root categories** on `/material`, which only became able to show a teaser earlier the same day when the card hook was extended to `material_types_landing`. **Result: 28 cards across `/material`, `/material/plants` and `/material/plants/shrubs` — 0 still showing the "At Brookstone Outdoors, our …" boilerplate**, verified by parsing the rendered live HTML of all three pages rather than trusting the write. Lengths 117–159 chars, inside the 300 guard. **The loader verifies before it writes:** six of these aliases are shared with another path in `{path_alias}`, so it resolves by alias and then confirms the result is a `material_types` term **whose name matches the copy**, reporting and skipping a mismatch instead of writing to the wrong page — the office owns the name, the script owns the copy. 23/23 matched, 0 problems. **⚠ The bigger item is NOT done and needs a file:** the root categories' **body** copy. **14 of 16** `field_public_description` values still open with the boilerplate (only **Backflow** and **Mulch** carry real copy, 374 and 242 chars). Marketing reports finished two-paragraph replacements for all sixteen have sat in **`Materials Landing Page Copy.md`** since late September, never pasted, plus a better `/material` header and footer — **that file has not been given to Code**. When it is, note its own warning: it predates the restructure, so its Trees / Shrubs / Plants / Annuals descriptions are for the old root positions and must not be used. **Also, a self-inflicted measurement artifact worth recording:** a second sitemap sweep at 8-way concurrency (with an earlier sweep still running, because I truncated the results file mid-run) produced **19 false 503s** and one `Cannot execute queries while other unbuffered queries are active` error. All 18 unique URLs returned **200** on sequential re-check — the site was fine; I was the load. Sweep gently (`-P 2`) against this box.

- **2026-10-03** — **⚠ The Fall Cleanup landing page was returning 500 — in fall cleanup season. Fixed (LIVE).** Reported by Todd while preparing a Search Console submission. `/services/landscape-lawn-care/yard-cleanup/fall-cleanup` threw `InvalidArgumentException: "0" is an invalid render array key` out of `Element::children()`, so anyone clicking a Google Ads link (`?c=goog26-fall`) or a service-page link got an error page. **Cause was a variable-name collision I introduced on 2026-09-28** adding the trust line to the campaign footers: `_bos_service_request_preprocess_fall_cleanup()` set **`bo_trust`** to the footer's "Licensed, Bonded & Insured" **string** and then, 27 lines later, **overwrote it with the hero's two-bullet ARRAY** — and the template *iterates* it in the hero (line 57) while *printing* it in the footer (line 130). The array won, and printing an array whose key `0` holds a string is exactly what the renderer refuses. Renamed so neither can shadow the other: **`bo_credentials`** (footer string) and **`bo_hero_stats`** (hero list), in one module and both templates, each with a comment saying why they are separate. **`/winterize` was immune for two reasons** — its template only ever prints the string, and its hero stats are hardcoded in markup — which is why the collision could surface on exactly one page. **My 9/28 verification is what let it ship:** I checked `/winterize`, `/winterize/week` and `/winterize/bear-creek` — "the three campaign pages" — while the changelog entry listed fall cleanup among the pages covered. It was never opened. Verified now: **6 requests, all 200, zero new PHP errors** (max `watchdog` wid unchanged across the batch), hero bullets and footer line both rendering, and the `?c=goog26-fall` ad landing works. **Then swept every URL in the live sitemap before the submission — 386 checked, 385 return 200.** The one exception was unrelated and pre-existing, and was **fixed the same hour on Todd's go-ahead**: `/about-us/our-equipment/cut-saw` **301'd to a page that is 403 to the public**, caused by a single `redirect` row (rid 36706) written on the TERM's own system path `taxonomy/term/1940` → `internal:/equipment/77683`. A redirect on a system path captures every request that resolves to that entity — including ones arriving by its own alias — so the page is unreachable at its canonical address. Fallout from the 2026-09-01 auto-title work (blank-titled `power_tools` record took an alias from its type term; fixing the title re-aliased it and left redirects behind, one on the term). **All 30,623 redirect rows scanned for the class — exactly 1**, isolated rather than systemic. Removed with `web/scripts/fix_term_system_path_redirect.php` (dry-run default; records each row so any can be recreated), leaving the entity's two legitimate old-alias redirects alone. Term page now 200, and the **sitemap re-swept clean at 386/386**. dblog retains only ~1 day here, so **nothing would have told us about the 500** — it was found by a human clicking the page five days later.

- **2026-10-03** — **Teasers for the four top-of-catalogue parents + the root landing wired up (LIVE).** Marketing supplied card teasers for **Plants, Trees, Shrubs and Evergreens** — the parents skipped while their children were written, where the old *"At Brookstone Outdoors, our … category brings year-round beauty"* boilerplate was most visible. **Checking them before pasting found two things.** (1) **The Plants teaser would not have rendered at all:** the Plants card lives on `/material`, which is the **`material_types_landing`** view, while yesterday's teaser hook only fired for **`material_children`** — so the hook now covers both. (2) **A factual conflict:** the Trees teaser read *"ornamentals under twenty feet"* while the Ornamental Trees page says *"under about twenty-five feet"* **twice** — a parent card contradicting its own child page is worse than either number, so it was loaded as twenty-five and the change recorded in the script, since it alters marketing's words. **The fallback is deliberately asymmetric between the two views, and measuring is what caught why:** the landing trims the body at **300** chars where `material_children` trims at **160**, so handing the landing our 160-char fallback would have **quietly shortened the card text of all 16 root categories that have no teaser**. On the landing, a category without a teaser is therefore left strictly to Views. **Verified over real HTTP, before and after, across four pages: 4 cards changed, 26 byte-identical** — Plants on `/material`, Trees + Shrubs on `/material/plants`, Evergreens on `/material/plants/trees`, and every other card unchanged to the byte. **Also corrected marketing's own list:** they guessed the untreated categories included **Deciduous**, which **already has a teaser and is live** — rewriting it would have replaced working copy. The real remaining set is **7, not 6**: Annuals · Ferns · Grasses · Groundcovers · Perennials · Roses · Vines (Roses on the Shrubs page, the rest on Plants), plus the **16 other root categories**, which can now carry a teaser for the first time. Their "already written" table was exactly right — those 11 are precisely the 11 cards that changed on 10-02. Lengths 127–155 chars, inside the 300 guard. Script `web/scripts/seed_parent_category_teasers.php` (dry-run default, `BOS_TEASER_APPLY=1`, alias-resolved, backup JSON).

- **2026-10-02** — **Category cards now print the line written for them (LIVE).** Surfaced while loading the Deciduous copy and shipped the same day on Todd's call. Marketing writes a purpose-built one-liner in `field_short_description` on every `material_types` term "for the card" — and **nothing rendered it**: `material_preprocess_views_view_field()` built the `material_children` child-card blurb from `field_public_description` **cut at 160 characters**, so a card opened with the first sentence of the essay, truncated mid-sentence, and often never said what the category was. Shade Trees read *"A shade tree is the highest-value plant decision anybody makes on a property, and it is the one with the longest tail. Everything else in a yard can be changed …"* where the written teaser reads *"The big ones — forty to seventy feet at maturity. The only thing you can plant that measurably cools a house…"*. **Material categories were the odd one out** — the same field is already the list teaser on five other BOS vocabularies (backflow device types + uses, spraying locations, plant characteristic categories, hardiness zones), which is exactly why marketing assumed it worked. Now: prefer the teaser, **fall back to the trimmed body only where no teaser exists**, so nothing regresses while the catalogue is filled in. The teaser renders **WHOLE** — several legitimate ones run past 160 (155–191 across this branch) and clipping a purpose-written one-liner is the bug being replaced; the 300-char cap is a guard against a pasted essay (14 of these terms arrived carrying 20–31 KB of pasted CSS on 2026-09-27), not a trim of intended copy. **11 cards changed text, 10 unchanged, 0 blank**, reported before/after per card (`web/scripts/report_material_card_blurbs.php`, read-only) — and it lit up teasers **already written and unused** for Pine, Spruce, Fir, Juniper, Arborvitae and both shrub categories, well beyond the branch that prompted it. **Latent bug fixed in the same function:** it stripped tags without decoding entities, so the **4** descriptions containing `&nbsp;` printed it literally at the reader (Christmas Lights, Sod, Pumps, Miscellaneous Materials — all body-fallback cards); decode now happens **after** `strip_tags` so an encoded tag can never become a real one, with an explicit replace for the decoded non-breaking space because `\s` does not match U+00A0. Verified by **rendering live as anon**: the three Deciduous cards print their teasers verbatim, 5 category pages 200, **0** `&nbsp;` leaks and **0** truncation ellipses anywhere. **⚠ Remaining is marketing's, not code's:** 10 categories still have no teaser and so fall back — most visibly **Evergreens, Trees, Plants and Shrubs**, whose cards still open with the old "At Brookstone Outdoors, our … category brings year-round beauty" boilerplate.

- **2026-10-02** — **Deciduous / Shade / Ornamental category copy reconciled — 3 fields changed, 16 already current (LIVE).** Marketing's 9/30 document for the Deciduous branch. **Diffed the stored copy field by field instead of reloading it** (the 2026-09-29 fruit-page lesson), and that was the whole job: **Deciduous Trees was already 100% identical**, Shade Trees was identical in substance, and only the **Ornamental Pear decision** was genuinely new. Applied: `Ornamental Trees.field_call_to_action` (+1,619 chars — the "And one tree we will sell you with a caveat" section, **+12 sentences and 0 lost**), `Ornamental Trees.field_teammate_description` (+583 — the two pear crew bullets, **0 lost**), and the **Ornamental Pear item's** `field_description` (477 → 1,894, replacing generic nursery copy — *"Cultivars such as 'Chanticleer' are selected for improved structure"* — with the honest included-bark/structural-pruning version). Previous values of all three saved to a backup JSON first. **The measurement that mattered:** five fields looked changed by length (Shade's CTA appeared to SHRINK by 193 chars and its crew copy by 366) and were **substantively identical** — the deltas were **CKEditor 5 `data-list-item-id` attributes** it stamps on every `<li>` when someone edits in the admin UI (~38 chars each, exactly 5 and 7 bullets' worth) plus inter-tag whitespace, since the stored copy is minified (`</h2><p>`) where the document has newlines. The loader now **normalises both away before comparing**, so it writes only real changes, never churns an office editor save, and is a no-op on re-run. **Three ad-hoc normalisers in a row gave misleading answers before I stopped and ran a real `git diff --word-diff` on dumped files** — the lesson being to diff files rather than keep writing comparison code. **Both of marketing's ⚠ items were already resolved and needed nothing:** the terms are already named "Shade Trees"/"Ornamental Trees" with the short `/shade` and `/ornamental` aliases intact, the stale `/deciduous/fruit-trees` link has **0 references** left (fixed 2026-09-29), and `/material/plants/trees/evergreens/juniper` **resolves** — all 6 paths in the copy verified against live. Terms resolved **by URL alias, never by name** (this branch has been renamed twice). Verified live as anon: all 5 pages 200, the ornamental page's four `<h2>`s in the documented order with the caveat section last, siblings sorting Shade → Ornamental → Fruit, and **0 crew-copy leak** on any of them. **⚠ Not loaded — no home for it:** the pear's item-level `field_teammate_description` block. `material` has only `field_description` and `field_care_instructions`, and **`field_care_instructions` is on the client display and anon-readable**, so putting crew sales language there would repeat the 2026-09-27 leak. Its operative instructions are already carried by the Ornamental Trees crew copy loaded above; the fuller 6-bullet item version needs a crew-only field on `material` (a small addition following the existing audience-tier pattern) — flagged, not invented. **⚠ Also surfaced (ROADMAP):** the category cards **never render `field_short_description`** — `material_preprocess_views_view_field()` truncates `field_public_description` to 160 chars instead, so every purpose-written teaser across this branch is stored and invisible. Script `web/scripts/seed_deciduous_tree_category_copy.php` (dry-run default, `BOS_COPY_APPLY=1`, alias-resolved, metatag-merging, format-preserving).

- **2026-10-02** — **Closed the remaining needless label deferrals — and the audit that found them was wrong by one (LIVE).** Follow-on to the placeholder work the same day: three bundles were still set to build their label on a **second** save that contrib runs in a PHP shutdown function, despite their patterns containing **no post-save token** — so any record created by a path that dies before shutdown (or any page rendered in the meantime) would show the raw `%AutoEntityLabel: <uuid>%`, exactly as WO#53970 did. **`equipment.heavy_equipment`, `media.wo_files` and `taxonomy_term.snow_plow_routes` flipped to `new_content_behavior: 0`** (surgical partial-cim of 3 files; **sync verified equal to live active on every key first**, per the never-full-cim rule). **The thing worth measuring, and the reason not to reason about it:** three of these patterns reach through a **reference chain** to another entity — `[media:field_work_order:entity:title] File: [media:field_media_file:entity:name]`, and snow routes walk user → `teammate_profile` → first name. Those chains **do** resolve while the entity itself is still unsaved (`WO#54031 - Weed Control File: 20190331_134846.jpg`; `Eunice - TEST ROUTE`), which is what makes behaviour 0 correct for them. **⚠ The audit over-counted, and the correction is the lesson:** I had listed **four** bundles by pattern-matching for `[…:id]`, but **`profile.teammate_profile` is not a candidate at all** — the `profile` entity type has **no label key**, so `AutoEntityLabelManager::hasLabel()` is FALSE and `auto_entitylabel_entity_presave()` **skips profiles entirely**. That config — pattern and all — is **dead and has never had any effect**; profiles are labelled by `Profile::label()` (`@type #@id`, overridable via `ProfileLabelEvent`). It was **deliberately left at 1**, because editing dead config implies it matters. Recorded as rule 4 of the gotcha: check the label key before counting a bundle as affected. New regression suite **`web/scripts/test_deferred_label_bundles.php`** — **11/11 on dev** (pre-save resolution + a real single save that stores the identical label, test records created and removed) and **7/7 on live** via `BOS_TEST_READONLY=1`, which **creates nothing in production**; the profile case asserts *inertness* rather than a working pattern, so the suite cannot fail permanently on an uncorrectable fact (the 2026-09-29 verifier lesson). **Live now reads 0 actionable deferrals** (97 correctly deferring on a `[…:id]` token, 1 inert) and **0 stored placeholders across 105 labelled entity types**; site 200, warm 0.018s.

- **2026-10-02** — **3,527 stored label placeholders healed on live in 6 seconds — and the method is the point (LIVE).** Surfaced by the WO#53970 controller fix earlier the same day; closed the same day. **3,436 `media:wo_images`** titles read `%AutoEntityLabel: <uuid>%` — exactly the count `wo_photo_split --apply` created on 2026-08-03, the run logged as *"hung after completing all work"* and killed. **That hang WAS the repair running:** contrib queues a per-record re-save in a PHP shutdown function, so 3,436 of them were grinding against S3 at ~2s each, and the kill discarded every one. Plus **91 `wo_status_updates`** whose titles embedded their **parent work order's** old placeholder, frozen in at creation. **Repaired by having auto_entitylabel's OWN decorator compute each label — so the text is byte-identical to what a save would write — then updating ONLY the label column** (`web/scripts/heal_media_label_placeholders.php`; dry-run default, `BOS_HEAL_TYPES=` required so nothing heals by accident, per-record guard that refuses to swap one unusable title for another, current revision row kept in step, cache tags invalidated). **A re-save was rejected for two concrete reasons, not a preference:** (1) the **enabled** pathauto pattern `work_order_media_groups` keys on `[media:name]`, and **0 of the 3,436 carry an alias today**, so 3,436 saves would have minted 3,436 aliases nobody asked for — plus a thumbnail read each against S3, ≈2 hours; (2) a `wo_status_updates` save **propagates status onto the parent work order**, which is the **2026-09-29 incident path** that knocked 17 invoiced WOs back to Scheduled. Column-write ran in **3.9s on dev, 6.1s on live**. **Proven inert, not assumed:** media rows 9,112 · wo_images 5,572 · image refs 8,653 · distinct files 8,637 · **media aliases 6,988 — every one identical before and after**; 0 data/revision row mismatches; sitemap still **488** public links with **0** media (media is not tracked, checked before touching anything); **0 work orders changed** and **0 status records created** in the window; and the two **Invoiced** parents among the 91 are **still Invoiced**. The media library renders real names ("Photos of Sprinkler System Repair") with **0** placeholders. Pre-flight also cleared the file-move risk (`retroactive_update: false`, filename template is `[file:fid]`, so no S3 file moves) and both hooks that fire on a media save (`properties_entity_presave` early-returns on media; `wo_photo_split` no-ops at one image — max is 1). **`media.wo_images` flipped to `new_content_behavior: 0`** (its pattern `Photos of [field_photos_of]` waits on nothing), verified by creating a record that labelled correctly on its first save. Dress-rehearsed end-to-end on dev, which carries the identical 3,436. Live DB dumped first (`~/pre-media-label-heal-20261002.sql.gz.gz`, 148M, gzip-verified). **⚠ Still open — prevention only, zero current damage:** four bundles defer a label with nothing to wait for (`equipment.heavy_equipment`, `media.wo_files`, `profile.teammate_profile`, `taxonomy_term.snow_plow_routes`), one config value each. **Correction worth recording:** my first propagation proof compared status fingerprints across two *different* WHERE clauses (39 parents vs 313) and so compared nothing — redone as "did any work order's `changed` move", which is the question that actually answers it.

- **2026-10-02** — **A crew saw `%AutoEntityLabel: …%` instead of the controller name — fixed at the source (LIVE).** Adding sprinkler information from WO#53970 produced `%AutoEntityLabel: 5b5d4e29-…%` as the **Controller(s)** heading. **Cause is a per-bundle SETTING, not the pattern**, and it is a second, independent cause of the sentinel beyond the documented `[entity:id]` one: `auto_entitylabel`'s **`new_content_behavior`** was **1** on `property_system_controller.controller`, which means *build the label on a second save* — and contrib runs that save from a **PHP shutdown function**, i.e. **after the response has already been sent**. So the page the crew is redirected to renders the raw placeholder, and the record then heals itself at shutdown — which is why an audit afterwards found **zero** stuck rows and nearly sent the hunt the wrong way (the forensic tell is the `changed` timestamp, not a surviving placeholder). The pattern is `#[field_controller_number] - [field_controller_type] Controller` — plain fields, both present on the first save — so **there was nothing to wait for**; of all the `property_*` sprinkler bundles this was the **only** one set that way. Flipped to **0** (surgical partial-cim of that one file; the import listed exactly one change). The quick-entry form also carries a scoped **`saveRecord()`** backstop so flipping the setting back in the UI cannot put the placeholder in front of a crew again. **The backstop has to re-read the STORED row, and that is the trap worth remembering:** `hook_entity_insert` repairs the label on the **in-memory** entity while leaving the database carrying the placeholder, so `$entity->label()` reads correctly while the row the next page renders is still wrong — my first guard checked the in-memory title, never fired, and **its test passed while the bug shipped**. A site-wide heal was **rejected**: **97** BOS bundles (work orders, estimates) legitimately defer because their pattern contains `[…:id]` and contrib already re-saves those, so a generic hook would add a **third** save across the system to fix a per-bundle misconfiguration. Verified live: WO#53970's record reads `#1 - Primary Controller`, and a fresh record gets its real title from a single save (test record created and deleted, nothing left behind); 6/6 on dev including the backstop under the broken setting. **⚠ Surfaced, NOT fixed (now a ROADMAP row):** a site-wide scan found **3,527 stored placeholders** — **3,436 `media:wo_images`**, exactly the count `wo_photo_split --apply` created on 2026-08-03, the run logged as *"hung after completing all work"* and killed. **That hang WAS the repair running**: contrib had queued 3,436 shutdown re-saves at ~2s each against S3, and the kill discarded all of them. Plus **91 `wo_status_updates`** whose titles embed their **parent work order's** old placeholder, frozen in at creation (all 91 parents are already healed, so these are stale copies) — and those must be repaired by **rewriting the title column directly, never by re-saving the entity**, because a status-update save propagates status onto the WO, which is the 2026-09-29 incident path. **0 URL aliases are affected.** Four more bundles defer with nothing to wait for and are latent instances of the same bug: `equipment.heavy_equipment`, `media.wo_files`, `profile.teammate_profile`, `taxonomy_term.snow_plow_routes`. New gotcha + the three rules in `Governance/drupal_bos_gotchas.md`; regression test `web/scripts/test_sprinkler_quick_label.php`. Commit `94cc21b1`.

- **2026-09-30** — **Route Editor moved onto an admin URL + a menu link (LIVE).** It had **no menu link at all** — reachable only by typing the address — and sat at **`/teammates/calendar/route-editor`** while being gated to office + supervisor with **no `teammates` role**, so the prefix was simply wrong (it also renders in the admin theme, which is why the URL jarred). Moved to **`/admin/office/work-orders/scheduling/route-editor`**, beside the sprinkler bulk scheduler already in this module, and linked as **Office ▸ Work Orders ▸ Scheduling ▸ Route Editor** (parent `bos_scheduling.scheduling_hub`). All four write/read endpoints moved with it; **nothing needed repointing** because every reference goes through `Url::fromRoute()` — the page hands the JS its URLs, so `dataUrl`/`assignUrl`/`reorderUrl`/`rescheduleUrl` followed automatically. **The old URL still works:** a `route_editor_legacy` route 301s it to the new one **carrying the query string**, so a bookmarked day/range lands where it did (verified: `?range=3&date=…` survives the redirect). Also linked from the **Scheduling hub** body as its own "Already scheduled" row rather than a fifth service card — the cards bulk-schedule what is *un*scheduled by service type, while the Route Editor arranges what is *already* scheduled, a different axis. **Two things worth recording.** (1) `menu_name` in a `links.menu.yml` **defaults to `tools`**, and all three of this module's links report `menu=tools` in their plugin definitions — but the stored tree resolves the menu from the **parent**, so they are genuinely in the **admin** menu. Confirmed by **rendering the admin tree** and finding the link, rather than "fixing" a non-problem by hardcoding `menu: admin`. (2) A **theme hook only receives variables declared in `hook_theme()`** — the hub's new `#route_editor_url` reached the template only after being added to `variables`; until then it silently vanished and the block rendered nothing (the href I first grepped was the new *menu* link on the same page, not the hub block — a false positive worth being careful of). Verified: new URL 200 with the controls, all four endpoint URLs moved, anon 403 + no-CSRF-token 403 on the new path, a real write through it, legacy 301 with query intact, hub renders the link, and the guard suite still **20/20**.

- **2026-09-29** — **Route Editor: move a stop's DATE from the map, and the lock rule that had to come with it (LIVE).** Tech reassignment already worked from the map; the date did not, so changing a day meant leaving the map for the scheduling form. Select stops — row checkbox, whole route, or **by clicking a pin** (the marker info window now carries a *Select this stop* control, built as a DOM node so it can hold a real listener) — then **Move** them to a day. The date is written in exactly **`ScheduleWriter`'s shape** (all-day smart-date span at **local** midnight, `duration 1439`) so a moved stop is indistinguishable from one the bulk scheduler created; midnight is computed in the **site timezone in PHP, never in SQL** (MariaDB runs UTC on this VPS while the site is America/Denver). `wo_schedule` back-fills the legacy daterange and logs the **Rescheduled** note. **Two things deliberately left alone:** route order (a moved stop keeps its sequence number and may collide with one already on that day — the receiving route's driving order is the office's call, one drag or Optimize away, and the confirm dialog says so) and `field_scheduled_firm`, so moving a stop we promised a customer never quietly un-promises it. **The rule, stated as a rule:** `LOCKED_STATUSES` = Complete 1097 / Warrantied 1283 / Invoiced 1281 / Paid 1504 / Canceled 1098, checked against the WO's **live** status on **all three** write endpoints (Move, Assign, reorder) — never a value the browser sent or the one baked into the page, because during this morning's incident six WOs were invoiced inside the window between two script runs and a clock-time cutoff got them wrong. Refusals come back as `blocked[]` naming the stop and its status, and the UI shows them in a dialog rather than reporting a silent partial success. **This closed a real hole rather than a hypothetical one:** `VISIBLE_STATUSES` **includes 1097 and 1283**, so Complete and Warrantied stops are on this map (the live week has two) — meaning **the existing Assign button could already do to a finished work order what this morning's renumber script did**, since any scheduling save fires `wo_schedule` → a "Scheduled" (1091) record back onto the WO. **Finished work: greyed in the list, never on the map.** A finished stop is listed in its day column (greyed, 🔒, status named, no checkbox, undraggable, skipped by select-all) so the office can see what the crew already knocked out, but it gets **no pin and the route line skips it** — so **Optimize** sequences only what's left (what you want mid-day) and the status line reads "N stops to run · M finished". All keyed on the payload's `locked` flag; the query still fetches `VISIBLE_STATUSES`, since filtering there would empty the list too. **Stop numbers stay positions in the FULL route** (pins read 1, 3, 4 with #2 done) — the gap says "that one's finished" and keeps map and list agreeing, which renumbering wouldn't. **Hover pairing had to be re-keyed on scheduling id:** a route with finished stops has fewer markers than rows, so the old positional `highlightRow(key, idx)`/`bounceMarker(key, idx)` would have bounced the wrong pin. The greying is presentation; **the guard is what holds** — greying decides what invites an edit, the guard decides what may be written, and only the guard sees current truth (a crew can sign off while the page sits open, and that POST is refused and named). Verified reversibly against live-synced data **20/20** (`test_route_editor_reschedule.php` — write shape, legacy-daterange sync, audit note, `2026-02-31` rejected, and refusal on all five closed statuses for both Move and Assign), plus real HTTP (anon 403, authed-without-CSRF-token 403, token accepted), then read-only on live (89 stops, 2 already locked). **⚠ Reported, not fixed:** `_wo_schedule_handle_status_update`'s date-change test reads `$entity->original`, which **is not populated on update** in this Drupal version, so `$changedDate` is **always TRUE** — *any* scheduling save, including an order-only reorder whose docblock claims it writes no note, emits a spurious "Scheduled for …" status record and re-flips `field_scheduled`. That is the mechanism behind this morning's incident; the new `wo_status_updates` guard stops it moving a closed WO's status, so what remains is audit noise. The fix is `loadUnchanged()`, but it touches **every** scheduling save (dispatch, sprinkler bulk tool, both winterize commands) and deserves its own verified pass. Also noted: that function's local constants `$STATUS_CANCELLED = 1097` / `$STATUS_COMPLETED = 1098` have the values **swapped** relative to BOS — behaviour is right, the names mislead. **Not built:** cross-column drag (drop a stop into another day/crew column = date + tech in one gesture) — it means one drop writing two tracked fields, so it wants its own pass. Docs: `Modules/route_editor.md`.

- **2026-09-29** — **⚠ INCIDENT: 17 invoiced work orders knocked back to Scheduled — repaired, and the guard that should have stopped it widened (LIVE).** `renumber_route_order.php` re-saved scheduling entities to repair route order. Saving one fires `wo_schedule`, which writes a **"Scheduled" (1091)** `wo_status_updates` record, and that module propagates the status onto the parent WO. **17 jobs crews had finished and the office had invoiced THAT MORNING were reset to Scheduled** (331 records, 13:46–14:04). **The 2026-07-11 resurrection guard exists for exactly this and did not fire: it named `1092` explicitly and said nothing about `1091`** — written against the symptom of that day (a clock-in reopening an invoiced WO) rather than against the rule. **Now absolute: a WO that is Complete / Invoiced / Paid / Warrantied / Canceled is never reopened by a status-update record, whatever status is written.** Enumerating which reopen was forbidden *was* the bug. Proved against the exact failure — re-saving a Complete WO's schedule now leaves it Complete. **Repair (`restore_reverted_wo_status.php`): the first pass got 6 of the 17 wrong**, restoring them to Complete, because it took "the last status before a CLOCK TIME" and the office had invoiced those six at 13:55, in the gap between my two script runs — caught only because Todd sent a screenshot of the status tab. Re-derived from **the last status entered by a HUMAN**, which needs no cutoff; all 17 now sit at Invoiced. Sign-off data was never touched (every `wo_complete_info` record survived, which is how the prior status was recoverable). **Lesson recorded:** a guard that enumerates forbidden transitions protects only the case its author had in mind; state the rule instead. **Separately, the check-up generator got an off switch and a settable window.** `hook_cron` called `_contract_residential_checkups_enqueue_dispatch()` unconditionally, and its two guards (one dispatch/day, don't stack while pending — the July 5.1M runaway fix) only prevent DUPLICATE dispatches, so there was **no way to stop generation short of editing code**. Added **`drush sset contract_residential.checkups_disabled 1`** (State, no deploy, **`--force` deliberately does NOT override** — "stop creating these" shouldn't have an exception a routine command trips over); **enabled on live at Todd's request** (it had generated 43 in September and dispatched again that day at 12:50). Also made the hardcoded **1 May – 15 Oct** irrigation window settable (`checkups_season_start` / `_end`, MM-DD), with malformed or impossible values falling back to the default rather than silently widening the season (verified: `banana` and `02-31` fall back, `02-29` accepted). Fixed the drush command's message, which returned *"Use --force to override"* for **all three** skip reasons including the one where force cannot help. Automatic generation only — the office can still create check-ups from the contract page via the existing VBO action.

- **2026-09-29** — **2026 winterizing season scheduled — 118 applied, route orders repaired, verifier made honest (LIVE).** Office needed a batch scheduled; the carry-forward from 21 Aug had covered 457 and the campaign/win-back bookings since had not been. `bos:winterize:plan` → `apply` put the remaining **118 on the calendar (29 Sep – 11 Nov)** — 96 with a tech, 22 unassigned (21 new customers proximity-filled, 1 inactive tech), attributed to uid 1, **notify suppressed**. Season now **586 WOs, 585 scheduled**, the one remainder Canceled. DB backed up first (`~/pre-winterize-apply-20260929.sql.gz`). **Three bugs of mine found and fixed, one of them caught only because the first pass was limited to 10.** (1) **`assignRouteOrder()` started its rank at 1 unconditionally** — right for the first run of a season, wrong for every run after: all ten of the trial batch landed on order 1 **on top of the existing first stop** for that date and tech. Now starts after the highest order already scheduled; verified **64 groups, zero collisions** before applying the other 108; the ten were then moved to the end of their routes by a script that touches **only those ten**, because renumbering a whole group would discard any route the office had arranged. Day bounds computed **in PHP against the raw timestamp** — `FROM_UNIXTIME` renders in MariaDB's session tz (fixed UTC−7 here) while the site is America/Denver, so a SQL date cast buckets local-midnight all-day records into the previous day for half the year; that is the 2026-07-14 calendar bug. (2) **Stale `$ordinal`** from the 08-30 calendar-date switch: warned per row and made `year_check` always read "differs: ordinal". (3) **44 (date,tech) groups had broken route order** — one tech had **thirteen stops all at 10**, a default not an arrangement, so the Route Editor had no order to show; likely the 08-30 re-dating moving 443 stops without renumbering them into their new groups. `renumber_route_order.php` fixes them **from today forward only** (the first dry run wanted **268** groups because it swept the whole season — **1,082 records are already behind us**, and next season's carry-forward reads historical order as a signal, so rewriting the past would feed it invented data); skips any group carrying `field_route_order_set` (verified the field exists with **zero** records, so that guard is real but untested); preserves relative order, id as tiebreak. **Scope note:** the renumber deliberately operates on **whole groups including non-winterizing stops** (32 check-ups, 13 weed-spray, 9 mowing, 5 repair share those days) — route order is per day per tech regardless of service, and half-renumbering a day is worse than either extreme. **The verifier was reporting three failures that were not failures**, which is worse than no verifier: it asserted the **nth-weekday rule replaced on 08-30**; asserted no scheduled WO is in an excluded status, which **cannot be judged after the fact** since nothing records when a status changed; and asserted the command's own records form a dense 1..N, which became wrong the moment the planner learned to append. Now: the calendar-date rule asserted **only on records nobody has edited since creation** (measured: **87 untouched, 0 deviate; 434 edited, 7 do**), status as an informational breakdown, and route density across the **whole day, today forward**, with past anomalies reported beneath so it cannot fail permanently on uncorrectable history. **7 passed, 0 failed.** ⚠ **Office:** assign the **22 unassigned** in the Route Editor, and run **Optimize** on the renumbered routes — where every stop was tied at 10 there was no sequence to preserve, so they are now in creation order: deterministic, but not geographically sensible.

- **2026-09-29** — **Subcategory pages now list their trees — `material_subcategory_items` + 59 trees filed (LIVE).** Closes the loop opened by `field_material_category`: Junipers 8 · Evergreens 16 · Shade 16 · Ornamental 13 · Fruit 6, each rendering on its own page, with every item URL nested and the old one 301-ing. **Why those pages looked empty, which is worth recording:** a BOS term page is **not** an entity page. The core **`taxonomy_term` view owns `/taxonomy/term/%`** and is a list of published **NODES** (`row: entity:node`, teaser) with the term itself rendered in its **header area** in view mode `full`. So a subcategory page was listing nodes — and materials are not nodes — which is why it rendered its copy and nothing else. The fix is an **EVA on `taxonomy_term:material_types`**, which lands inside the term's own render in that header beside the existing `material_children` and `material_type_items`; the core view is deliberately untouched, since it governs roughly **1,300** term pages. Cloned from `material_characteristic_items` with the argument moved to `field_material_category_target_id`; DISTINCT, header area `empty: FALSE` so the heading appears only when the category holds something, and no empty-area message. Heading is static ("What we stock") because the page H1 already carries the category name. Parent pages correctly show **0** from this view — nothing points directly at Trees or Deciduous Trees, which keep their bundle listing and their child-category cards. **The 59 assignments** (`assign_tree_categories.php`) were split into two groups on purpose: **botany** (evergreens, junipers, fruit — objective) applied first, and the **shade/ornamental split** held for Todd because it is a merchandising judgement rather than a property of the plant. Todd approved it and decided the three genuinely open ones — **Birch → Shade, Japanese Pagoda Tree → Shade, Thinleaf Alder → Ornamental** — now recorded in the script so a re-run reproduces them. The script never overwrites a category the office has set (it reports and moves on) and resolves every category term **by relationship**, since these have been renamed twice. **⚠ Open:** **Eastern Red Cedar** is the one tree left unfiled, pending Todd's call on whether to add a *Cedar* category. Recommendation against: the whole catalogue holds only **two** cedar-adjacent items (it and Arborvitae, already in Evergreens), so the category would have one member; "cedar" is a common name spanning *Juniperus*, *Thuja*, *Calocedrus* and *Cedrus*, which would put a third organising principle into a list that already mixes growth form with genus; and it has a correct home already — **Rocky Mountain and One-Seed Juniper are in Junipers and are upright native trees of the same kind**, which is the fact that settles it. Also still to do: the field on **`shrubs`** (Roses).

- **2026-09-29** — **Fruit pages reconciled against marketing's revised document (LIVE).** Asked to confirm the two fruit pages were current after Chat revised the copy. Diffed the STORED copy field-by-field against the document rather than reloading it wholesale — which mattered, because reloading verbatim would have **re-broken a link**: the document still carries the pre-rename path `/material/plants/trees/deciduous/fruit-trees`, while the live alias is now `/…/fruit`. **Everything matched except one thing** — the footer was missing the new **"We prune and spray them too"** section, which is the section that answers the question left open when the page was built (whether fruit tree pruning and spraying is a service we offer — it is). Inserted verbatim, positioned before "Fruit as a landscape decision…" per the document's order, by `fruit_03_prune_spray_section.php` (idempotent; dry-run default; refuses rather than guesses if the anchor heading is missing). Verified live as anon: five `<h2>`s in the documented order, the new body present, both cross-links 200, **0** crew-copy leak on either page. **The structural finding worth keeping:** marketing writes these as "VIEW HEADER" and "VIEW FOOTER", and BOS already has exactly that shape on `material_types` — `field_public_description` (weight 0) renders above the lists and **`field_call_to_action`** (weight 20) below them, with the `material_children` / `material_type_items` EVAs at weight 10 between. So header/footer copy belongs on the TERM, not on a view: a subcategory items view is shared by every subcategory and could not carry per-term copy. **⚠ Name-matching fragility, twice in one day:** the office renamed this category **Fruit Trees → Fruit → Fruit Trees** and its parent **Deciduous → Deciduous Trees**. `fruit_02_content.php` matched BOTH by exact name, so it had silently become a script that would abort on the parent and create a **duplicate** child; both lookups now match on the relationship and a name prefix, and the new script does the same from the start. Standing lesson, same as the credential seeder: **a script may own creating a record, but the office owns its name — never match on one.** Also recorded on the ROADMAP: marketing's **Jan–Mar fruit-tree-pruning campaign**, which is the first item here with a real deadline (outreach lands early December → list pulled in November → **decide before November**), gated on how many people can actually prune a fruit tree; plus the six fruit tree children and a separate fruit-tree *care* service page.

- **2026-09-29** — **Trees get a subcategory, and it goes in the URL — `field_material_category` + a pathauto floor (LIVE).** A tree's page can now sit at **`/material/plants/trees/deciduous/fruit/apple-tree`**, so trimming the URL back lands on Fruit, then Deciduous, then Trees, then Plants — all four verified 200. **Modelled on `decorative_rock`, which already does this, but it could NOT use rock's token, for a structural reason worth recording:** rock's pattern inserts the term **name** as one segment (`[material:field_rock_type:entity:name]`), and that works only because **`rock_types` is FLAT** — every term's real alias is `/material/rock/{name}`, so trimming lands on the term page (checked: `/material/rock/river-rock` is 200). **`material_types` is NESTED** — Fruit lives under Deciduous — so the name-only token would have produced `/material/plants/trees/fruit/…` and trimming back to that would **404**, which is the exact behaviour the URL exists to provide. The pattern therefore uses the term's **own alias**: `[material:field_material_category:entity:url:path]/[material:title]`. Second benefit: the hierarchy is no longer restated in the pattern, so moving a category carries its items along, instead of needing patterns hand-edited the way Trees and Shrubs did when they moved under Plants. (Verified the mechanism first — `material_supplier_info_path` already builds aliases this way, and `[term:url:path]` on Fruit returns the full nested alias.) **The empty case was measured, not assumed:** with no category the token clears and the alias collapses to **`/{title}` at the site root** (rock is immune because it hardcodes `material/rock/` in front of its token). Rather than accept the weaker shape, the floor lives in code — **`material_pathauto_alias_alter()`** rebuilds any material alias that escaped `/material/` from that bundle's **own** `material_types` term (the one whose `field_material_bundle` names the bundle), resolved at run time so the fallback follows the taxonomy rather than restating it. **That makes it safe to ship BEFORE the data entry:** an unassigned tree keeps exactly the URL it has today. Proven on live read-only across **all 60 trees — 0 would change**; on dev, assigning Fruit to Apple Tree moved it to the nested URL with the old one **301**-ing, and clearing it moved it back. Field is **not required** (a required field would block the office saving a tree they have not categorised yet) and renders as a select, which indents the nesting. Scripts: `setup_material_category_field.php` (idempotent, bundle-parameterised — `shrubs` is one constant away). **Fallout from the office renaming "Fruit Trees" → "Fruit"** handled in the same pass: (1) `fruit_02_content.php` matched that term by **exact name**, so a re-run would have found nothing and created a **second** fruit category — now matches on the relationship (a child of Deciduous named `Fruit*`) and sets the name only on create, the same lesson as the credential seeder; (2) the cross-link in the **Fruit-Bearing** characteristic copy still carried `/…/deciduous/fruit-trees` — repointed by `fix_fruit_category_link.php`, which resolves the category's **current** alias at run time rather than hardcoding the new one (the old category URL still 301s, so nothing was broken — but an internal link should not need a redirect). **Note for next time:** the first scan for that stale link returned a **false negative** — it swept `taxonomy_term__*` field tables, and these vocabularies keep their public copy in the **core `description`** column of `taxonomy_term_field_data`, which is not a field table. **⚠ Remaining (ROADMAP):** the subcategory **listing** is not built, so assigning a category moves the item's URL but the Fruit page still lists nothing — it needs a clone of `material_characteristic_items` keyed on `field_material_category`; `shrubs` still lacks the field; and the ~60 assignments are office work.

- **2026-09-29** — **Characteristic pages now list their plants — `material_characteristic_items` EVA (LIVE).** `field_plant_characteristics` (material → `plant_characteristics`) was **already populated** — 28 of the 41 characteristics carry tagged plants, Flowering 30 / Foliage Interest 26 / Bird-Attracting 24 / Fruit-Bearing 13 — but **no view rendered them**, so every characteristic page was header + footer copy sitting over invisible data. Checking the rendered pages, not the config, is what surfaced it: `/…/flowering` and `/…/bird-attracting` each returned **zero** view wrappers and zero item links. New EVA attached to `taxonomy_term:plant_characteristics`, contextual argument = the current **term id**. **Cloned at runtime from `material_type_items`** (the listing on a `material_types` CATEGORY page) so a characteristic page looks like the category pages beside it — same table, same columns, same `thumbnail` style — following the `build_material_children_view.php` house pattern (`createDuplicate()` + deltas, so each env clones its own local sibling). **Four deltas, each with a reason:** (1) argument → `field_plant_characteristics_target_id`, and the EVA passes `argument_mode: id` rather than the source view's token `[term:field_material_bundle:value]`, which a characteristic term does not carry; (2) **`query.distinct: TRUE`** — the argument joins a multi-value field table, where the source view's argument was a single-value base column; (3) the heading is a **header area with `empty: FALSE`**, so it renders only when the term actually has plants; (4) **no empty area at all** — an untagged characteristic renders nothing, rather than a heading over "No materials found", which is what `/material/plants` still does (its header is `empty: TRUE`, recorded on the ROADMAP). Heading is deliberately **static** ("Plants with this characteristic"): the argument is a term ID, so a token would print the number, and the term's own H1 already names the characteristic directly above. The EVA auto-enabled at weight 10 on **all five** displays (`default`/`full`/`client_view`/`teammate_view`/`admin_view`) — last, after the descriptions — which is correct: the plant catalogue is public and every audience should see it. Verified live as **anon by rendering**: Flowering **30** unique plants (no duplicates — 60 links = image + name per row), Bird-Attracting 24, Fruit-Bearing 13, all linked with thumbnails and descriptions; an untagged characteristic renders **only an empty wrapper** (no heading, no message); the **category** pages (`/…/aesthetic-features`) and `/material/plants/shrubs` are untouched; **0** cost, supplier or crew-copy leakage on any of them. Script `build_material_characteristic_items_view.php` (idempotent — deletes and rebuilds; guards on the vocabulary and the field table existing). Deploy: scp script → run → `cr` (no cim/DB). Commit `6b1b9f12`. **⚠ Reported, not fixed** (all on the ROADMAP's new *Plant catalogue* section): **subcategory pages list nothing** — Evergreens / Junipers / Deciduous / Shade / Ornamental / **Fruit Trees** are all header-and-footer over an empty list, because a `material_types` term binds to items only via `field_material_bundle` → the material's BUNDLE, and no subcategory has one (there is no `fruit_trees` bundle); fixing it needs a category reference field on `material` plus a listing view, then data entry. Also: **only Cherry Tree is tagged Fruit-Bearing** — Apple/Apricot/Peach/Pear/Plum Tree are in the catalogue but untagged, so they are missing from the page built to list them (office tagging, not code).

- **2026-09-28** — **Breadcrumbs extended to all 25 public catalogue/reference vocabularies; dark-variant experiment reverted; footer lightened (LIVE).** **(1) Breadcrumbs:** `/material/plants/characteristics/…/flowering` showed only **"Home"**. Root cause: the vocabulary is **flat** — every term is a root term — while the URL *looks* hierarchical because pathauto synthesises it from a field (`/material/plants/characteristics/[term:field_characteristic_category]/[term:name]`). `field_characteristic_category` is a plain **list field, not a parent reference**, so core's hierarchy-based builder finds no parent and stops. That mismatch is precisely what `bos_breadcrumbs` exists for; the vocab simply was not on its allowlist. Added **25 vocabularies** (`plant_characteristics` 41, `growth_zone` 26, `spraying_locations` 20, `weed_categories` 10, `rock_types`/`wind_direction` 9 each, …) = **187 term pages** now carrying a real trail. **Kept as an allowlist**, never flipped to "everything except operational" — ~1,300 operational term pages are publicly indexed and must not grow breadcrumbs by accident. **Verified by rendering a sample page from every one of the 25 and checking each individual crumb target resolves** (not merely that a trail appeared): 24 clean. **Finding:** the category segment renders even though the alias table has **no entry** for it — it resolves as a **route**, which the builder also checks. **⚠ Reported, not fixed:** `irrigation_check_up_frequency`'s 4 terms still carry aliases on the **old** parent segment `/services/sprinkler-system/sprinkler-system-check` (re-aliased to `…/check`, redirect left behind), so that crumb **301s** rather than resolving directly — works, but correcting it means changing 4 live public URLs. **(2) Dark variant REVERTED.** Built earlier the same day, then removed: it was meant for dark-mode devices but on a dark-mode system it applied in every standards-honouring browser, which was not wanted. All `prefers-color-scheme` blocks removed from 4 stylesheets; `color-scheme` back to **`light`** in CSS + meta — **`light dark` was itself part of the problem**, since it tells the browser the page supports dark and it then renders its own surfaces (canvas, form controls, scrollbars) dark before our CSS is involved. **Samsung Internet finding (researched, not guessed):** it **ignores `prefers-color-scheme` entirely** — renders the *light* CSS and applies a proprietary filter — and ignores `color-scheme` too, so the dark variant was **inert there** and merely handed the filter darker input. Per Samsung's own developer forum there is **no developer-side bypass**. **(3) Footer lightened instead** — the one lever that helps both normal and force-darkened rendering, since the filter darkens proportionally: body `#D9CEC0 → #EFE8DE` (11.6→**14.8:1**), muted `#A99A88 → #C8BBA9` (6.6→**9.5:1**), hours `#B4A99D → #D3C9BD` (7.8→**11.0:1**). **Deploy gotcha re-encountered:** the live module file read `light` while the rendered page still served `light dark` — **stale `lsphp` workers**; `drush cr` does not clear them, `pkill -u brookstoneadmin lsphp` then `cr` does (already documented 2026-09-20). New governance section **"Look it up; don't recall it"** added to `working_with_claude.md` after three wrong confident diagnoses on one problem.

- **2026-09-28** — **Footer/dark-mode contrast + a palette-wide WCAG audit (LIVE).** Reported as "too dark on a dark-mode display". **Measuring rather than guessing found two separate causes.** **(1) The page never declared a colour scheme** — no `color-scheme` in any CSS or the HTML — so a browser's **automatic dark mode** is free to re-colour it algorithmically, and on a footer that is *already* dark by design that pass muddies it (which is why the rendered footer looked far dimmer than its declared 9.64:1). Every colour in this design is an explicit hex and nothing derives from the system scheme, so **`color-scheme: light`** (CSS `:root` + a `<meta>` emitted sitewide from `bos_service_request_page_attachments`) changes nothing about how the page renders — it only opts out of a re-colouring the design never needed. **(2) Three footer colours genuinely failed AA**, and they were exactly the dimmest items in the screenshot: copyright / privacy link / tagline / lineage **#8A7A69 4.34:1 → `--bo-footer-muted` #A99A88 6.56:1**; phone number **#CB6015 4.47:1 → `--bo-footer-accent` #E3813A 6.39:1**; body text lifted for comfort **#C9BCAA 9.64 → #D9CEC0 11.59**. **Scoped to the footer with its own tokens** rather than changing `--bo-muted-2`/`--bo-orange` globally — those are correct on light pages and the failure is specific to the ink ground. **(3) Follow-on, palette-wide audit** against all four light grounds (paper/card/sand/white): `--bo-prose`, `--bo-text-2`, `--bo-muted`, `--bo-green` **pass**; **`--bo-muted-2` #8A7A69 failed everywhere (3.45–4.14:1)** → **#736555** (4.7 sand / 5.6 card). Only two uses outside the footer, both small body text (form helper text, character-limit note) — the step below `--bo-muted` is now carried by **font size** rather than colour. **Still outstanding (raised, not decided):** `--bo-orange` #CB6015 as **body-link text** is 3.34–4.02:1 on light grounds — real AA failures on `.legal-page a`, `.about-page .about-sec a`, `.about-hero__call a`, `.home-card__more`, `.state-county-card .views-field-view a`, `.about-kicker`. Fix would mirror the footer: a `--bo-orange-text` (#A54E10 ≈ 4.7–5.7:1) for text only, brand #CB6015 kept for buttons, borders, separators and the 38px stat — but that changes the resting colour of every link on the public site, so it wants an explicit yes. `--bo-blue` #62B5E5 (1.89–2.27) is accent-only, not text.

- **2026-09-28** — **Founding year corrected 1995 → 1997, including the claims derived from it (LIVE).** Ten places said 1995, across content, config, JSON-LD and hardcoded copy. **Targeted, not a global replace** — "1995" is also a street address (**9103 1995 Rd., Austin**), a work-order number, a contract id, a teammate's birthday and a crew note about an irrigation clock resetting itself. **Changed:** `/about-us` + `/services/landscape-lawn-care/maintenance-program` bodies, the sitewide footer lineage line (`block_content 136`), the `/services` landing intro (view header), `metatag.metatag_defaults.front` description + `.global` og_description, **both `foundingDate` values** in the LocalBusiness and Organization JSON-LD, the `/about` page `<title>`, and the winterize + fall-cleanup meta descriptions. **Also the arithmetic:** 2026 − 1997 = **29**, so every **"30+ years" / "over 30 years"** stopped being true the moment the year moved — leaving them would have put a contradiction beside the fix. They now read **"since 1997"** rather than a smaller number, so they never go stale (homepage trust bullet, winterize hero stat, Winterizing term subtitle + summary, two module meta descriptions). **One sentence needed judgement, not arithmetic:** `/about-us` said *"For 30 years, Steve and Eunice built the business"*, which beside *"began in 1997"* reads as **2027**; their actual tenure isn't derivable from anything in BOS and isn't ours to invent, so it now says **"For decades"** — office can put the real figure in. **Left alone deliberately:** Steve Ward's bio ("over 32 years of experience in the Green Industry") — his own career, not the company's age. Scripts: `audit_founding_year.php` (read-only, scans all **6,562** text columns + flags derived claims), `fix_founding_year.php` (dry-run default, `BOS_YEAR_APPLY=1`, backup JSON). **Verified live: 15 public pages, zero "1995" and zero "30 years", all 200.** **Script gotcha recorded:** an **arrow function captures by value**, so a `&$counter` passed into a closure from inside `array_map(fn() => …)` never comes back — the config edit silently reported "nothing to change"; use a `foreach`, or compare the arrays.

- **2026-09-27** — **Service pages show their own reviews + `review.css` was never attaching (LIVE).** A review already records its service (`field_testimony_service`), so the Repair one now renders on **`/services/sprinkler-system/repair`** — where someone deciding whether to call is actually standing — not only on a reviews page nobody navigates to. New **`service_testimonials`** EVA on `taxonomy_term:services`, same shape as `service_children`: the **term id is the contextual argument**, so it needs no per-term setup and lights up for each service as reviews arrive. **Exact match only** — a Repair review does **not** appear on its parent Sprinkler Systems; rolling up would mean feeding the argument a term **plus its descendants** from a hook (the `equipment_similar` approach), and a parent page quietly claiming its children's reviews is a different promise to the reader, so that stays a deliberate next step. **Status filter operator is `or`, NOT `in`** — on a list_string field the handler is ManyToOne, which has **no `in` operator**, and `in` emits **no SQL at all**, which here would publish every **pending** review on a public page; verified with a pending record present that it does not appear. Heading names the service and is **removed entirely** when that service has no approved reviews (an empty "What customers say" reads worse than nothing). **Live bug fixed alongside:** `review.css` was only ever attached by the `/review` controller, so **`/about-us/reviews` had been rendering the card classes with no stylesheet behind them** — `hook_views_pre_render()` now attaches it for both views. Verified live anon: Repair renders heading + quote + attribution, Aeration renders neither, `review.css` loads on all three. Script `build_service_testimonials_view.php`, test `test_service_testimonials.php` (renders the term page as anon, with an approved *and* a pending record).

- **2026-09-27** — **Testimonial URLs → `/about-us/testimonial/ID0002`, image filed to match (LIVE).** The alias pattern was `/[testimonial:field_customer:entity:url:path]/[service]-testimonial-[id]`, and **`field_customer` is a USER reference** — so a testimonial with a customer linked got `/client/john-smith/repair-testimonial-4`. The page is **403 to anon** so that was never reachable, **but the image filed alongside it would have been**: a file in `public://` is fetchable whatever the entity access says (the old image pattern `[testimonial:uid:entity:url:path]/testimonials` was worse still — it named the file after the **author**, producing `public://client/abbey-gilmore/testimonials/abbey-gilmore_…` for an office-entered note). Both now key on a **padded reference**: new **`[testimonial:ref]`** token → `ID0002` (same shape as the backflow device's `bf_number`) — no name, no service, no customer, **stable** (entity id never changes) and it sorts. Image becomes `about-us/testimonial/ID0002.png`, so page and picture obviously match. **Pathauto lowercases aliases site-wide**, so the stored alias is `id0002` — left alone, since that setting governs every alias on the site; the collation is `utf8mb4_general_ci`, so a printed/typed **`ID0002` resolves anyway** (verified live: **403 not 404** for both cases). Old alias **301s automatically** (redirect module + `update_action=2`) — verified `/repair-testimonial-2` → `/about-us/testimonial/id0002`. **Existing files are NOT moved** (`retroactive_update` off) — nothing gained, live URLs would break. Script `set_testimonial_paths.php` (idempotent, per-env — pathauto patterns and aliases don't ride cim), test `test_testimonial_paths.php`. Commit `c76b06b5` ff. **Test-harness gotcha worth remembering:** `FileSystem::prepareDirectory()` takes its argument **by reference**, so a string literal is a fatal — assign to a variable first (the shipped code always did; two throwaway test scripts did not).

- **2026-09-27** — **"Read the note" — vision transcription of handwritten reviews + office-set blur (LIVE, `bos_testimonial`).** Office is entering a backlog of handwritten thank-you cards. **(1) Transcription (works, shipped):** a **Read the note** operation on any testimonial carrying a scan sends it to the **vision provider already configured for the invoice importer** (`ai` + `ai_provider_anthropic`, claude-sonnet-4-5 — no new dependency) and drafts the words into `field_testimony` plus the name into `field_testimonial_by`. Prompt forbids tidying the wording, marks unreadable words `[?]` rather than inventing them, and **excludes the signature/address/phone from the transcript**. Verified end-to-end **on live**: transcribed a note verbatim in **6.2s**, `signed_by` correct, and all three personal lines absent from the transcript. **(2) Redaction — built differently than asked, and the reason is the point.** The obvious build is model bounding boxes → blur them. It **returns** boxes; on a 700px test note they were **~190px high**, so the blur covered *"We will be calling you again next season"* while the **signature, street address and phone stayed perfectly readable** — beneath an image that now *looked* redacted. That is **worse than no blur**, because the redacted look is what earns the trust, and padding cannot absorb an error that size without blurring most of the note. So the model's findings became a **checklist** ("signature after Sincerely", "phone at the bottom") and the office sets **one horizontal line** with a live preview — everything below it blurs. One number covers a signature block, and the person setting it can **see** whether it worked. **Verified:** at 70% the signature/address/phone each drop **228.7 → ~18–22** contrast and are visually smears with no letterforms while the message stays crisp; at **80% the signature survives at 228.7** — exactly the feedback the preview exists to give. Blur **pixelates at a large block before smoothing** (discards detail rather than softening it — a gaussian alone can be partly undone); preview carries an orange outline the saved copy does not; **nothing reaches `field_testimonial_image` without the checkbox ticked after looking at the result**. GD only (imagick is **not** installed on live). New: `NoteVisionExtractor`, `NoteRedactor` (`redactBand()`), `ReadNoteForm` (full page, not a modal — the material-import lesson; services **declared not promoted** so the rebuilding form survives serialisation). Route `/testimonial/{id}/read-note`, `_entity_access: testimonial.update` (anon **403**). **(3) Form reorder:** Testimony had been **seventh**, under Status, Work order, Submitter email and both images — now first, with Testimonial by, the scan and the public image behind it. **Dev has a placeholder Anthropic key**, so the vision half is provable only on live; the GD half is env-independent and passes on both. Scripts: `order_testimonial_form.php`, plus diagnostics `test_note_vision.php` / `test_note_redactor.php` / `test_note_band.php`. Commits `f2ec5a35`, `643df36e`.
